y^{\prime}\in\ker\varphi_{B_{M}}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}}
. Hence
\iota_{*}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}}|
is surjective as required.
This completes the proof of the claim that
\iota_{*}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}}|
is an isomorphism.

It follows that for a
4
-manifold
X
that is
\mathbb{CP}^{2}
-stably homeomorphic to
M
,
the kernel of
\varphi_{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B_{M};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B_{M};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
contains a
\mathbb{Z}/2
subgroup that injects into
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi]}H_{4}(B_{M};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi])
under
\red_{2}^{M}
if and only if, writing
B_{X}:=P_{2}(X)
, the kernel of
\varphi_{X}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
contains a
\mathbb{Z}/2
subgroup that injects into
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi]}H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi])
under
\red_{2}^{X}
.

By
Lemma

3.9
,
c_{*}([M])=0\in H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
, for a classifying map
c\colon M\to B\pi
inducing an isomorphism on fundamental groups. Suppose we have a
4
-manifold
X
with fundamental group
\pi
and
c_{*}([X])=0\in H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
Then such an
X
is
\mathbb{CP}^{2}
-stably homeomorphic to our
M
by
[
44
]
*Theorem 1.2 (which is due to Kreck
[
51
]
; however the citation we provided gives the explicit statement).
So it suffices to show that the kernel of
\varphi_{B_{X}}
contains a
\mathbb{Z}/2
subgroup that injects into
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi]}H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi])
under the change of coefficients map
\red_{2}^{X}
, and then by the previous paragraph we deduce the analogous fact for
M
.

To construct such a
4
-manifold, let
X
be a
double
for
\pi
and orientation character
w
, obtained by definition as the boundary of a
5
-dimensional thickening
W
of the standard
2
-complex corresponding to a presentation for the group
\pi
, with the thickening chosen to have orientation character
w
. Since
X
is the boundary of a
5
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
, and therefore
(c_{X})_{*}([X])=0\in H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
under a classifying map
c_{X}\colon X\to B\pi
. By
[
8
]
*Theorem 1, the first
k
-invariant of a 2-complex
K
is trivial if and only if
\pi_{1}(K)
has cohomological dimension at most 2. Thus the
k
-invariant of
W
, and therefore of
X
, is nontrivial. By
[
43
]
*Lemmas 7.11, 7.12, and 7.14,
\pi_{2}(X)\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{v}
. Hence
B_{X}
is homotopy equivalent to a CW complex
B
as in
Lemma

7.5
, and we deduce from that lemma that the kernel of the map
\varphi_{X}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
contains a
\mathbb{Z}/2
subgroup that injects into
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi]}H_{4}(B_{X};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi])
under the change of coefficients map.
This completes the proof of the corollary.
∎

7.3.
Lower bounds on the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
for
3
-manifold fundamental groups that are free products

With the results of the previous subsection in hand, we can now prove the following result bounding the size of the kernel of the map
\varphi_{B}
, for
B
the Postnikov
2
-type of a
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
as in (
7.1
). First we need a lemma showing how the kernel of the reduction of coefficients map
\varphi
changes under stabilisation.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
. Let
B:=P_{2}(M)
and let
B^{s}:=P_{2}(M\#(S^{2}\times S^{2}))\simeq P_{2}(M\vee S^{2}\vee S^{2})
. The map
M\to M\vee S^{2}\vee S^{2}
induces an isomorphism between
\ker\varphi_{B}
and
\ker\varphi_{B^{s}}
.

Proof.

The
E^{2}_{0,4}
term of the Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration
\widetilde{B}\to B\to B\pi
converging to
H_{*}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
is
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})^{w})\cong H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{w})
.
Quotienting by the images of the iterated differentials in the spectral sequence with codomain the terms
E^{k}_{0,4}
induces a map
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{w})\to F_{0,4}^{B}
. The codomain
F_{0,4}^{B}
is then a subgroup of the output
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
of the spectral sequence, and, under the identification
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, the kernel is precisely the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
. We have a similar quotient map
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{w})\to F_{0,4}^{B^{s}}
for
B^{s}
. The idea of the proof is to compare the kernels of these two maps using naturality of the Leray-Serre spectral sequence.

The inclusion map
M\to M\vee S^{2}\vee S^{2}
and the collapse map
M\vee S^{2}\vee S^{2}\to M
induce isomorphisms on fundamental groups, and hence induce isomorphisms between
\pi_{1}(B)
and
\pi_{1}(B^{s})
. These maps also induce respectively the inclusion
\pi_{2}(B)\cong\pi_{2}(M)\to\pi_{2}(M)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi^{2}\cong\pi_{2}(B^{s})
and the projection
\pi_{2}(B^{s})\cong\pi_{2}(M)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi^{2}\to\pi_{2}(M)\cong\pi_{2}(B)
on second homotopy groups. We have that
H_{p}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}\pi)=0
for all
p>0
, and that
H_{3}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H_{3}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})=0
, and similarly for
B^{s}
. The latter claim holds because
0=\pi_{3}(\widetilde{B})\to H_{3}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
is surjective in degree three by the Hurewicz theorem. It follows that the induced maps
B\to B^{s}
and
B^{s}\to B
induce isomorphisms on
H_{p}(\pi;H_{q}(-;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
for all
p>0
and all
q<4
. Whence naturality of the Leray-Serre spectral sequence implies that the iterated images of the differentials in
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(-;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{w})
and its iterated quotients, for
B
and
B^{s}
, are identified by the inclusion and collapse maps. Thus the kernels of
\varphi_{B}
and
\varphi_{B^{s}}
are isomorphic as claimed.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
as in (
7.1
), and orientation character
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
, such that
(\pi,w)
is admissible.
Let
t^{\prime}\leq t
be such that, for some identification as in (
7.1
), the image of the fundamental class
[M]
in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\bigoplus_{k=1}^{t}H_{4}(H_{k};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{t}
is trivial in the first
t^{\prime}
summands and nontrivial for
k>t^{\prime}
.
Let
B:=P_{2}(M)
. Then the kernel of
\varphi_{B}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
is an abelian group that needs at least
t^{\prime}
generators.

Proof.

As
M
is stably a connected sum by
Lemma

3.13
,
there exists
m\geq 0
and a connected sum decomposition

M\#\bighash^{m}S^{2}\times S^{2}\cong N\#M_{1}\#\cdots\#M_{t}

with
\pi_{1}(N)\cong F*\big(\ast_{i=1}^{r}Z_{i}\big)*\big(\ast_{j=1}^{s}G_{j}\big)
and
\pi_{1}(M_{k})\cong H_{k}\cong\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
. Let
B^{s}:=P_{2}(M\#m(S^{2}\times S^{2}))\simeq P_{2}(M\vee\bigvee^{2m}S^{2})
. The map
M\to M\vee\bigvee^{2m}S^{2}
induces a map
B\to B^{s}
. By
Lemma

7.8
, this map induces an isomorphism
\ker\varphi_{B}\xrightarrow{\cong}\ker\varphi_{B^{s}}
. Hence it suffices to prove the statement of the lemma with
B^{s}
in place of
B
.

Let
\pi^{\prime}:=\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2=\langle t,T\mid[T,t],T^{2}\rangle
and
v^{\prime}\colon\pi^{\prime}\to C_{2}
be given by
v^{\prime}(t)=1
and
v^{\prime}(T)=-1
. Let
\overline{w}
denote the composition
\pi^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
, where the first map is the inclusion as one of the factors in (
7.1
). Let
v=\overline{w}v^{\prime}
.
Let
M^{\prime}
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi^{\prime}:=\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
and
\pi_{2}(M^{\prime})\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{v}
. Let
B^{\prime}
be the Postnikov
2
-type of
M^{\prime}
.

For each factor
H_{k}
of
\pi
,
k=1,\dots,t^{\prime}
, by
Lemma

3.9
there are maps
B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\iota_{k}}B^{s}
and
B^{s}\xrightarrow{p_{k}}B^{\prime}
such that
p_{k}\circ\iota_{k}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{B^{\prime}}
and
p_{k}\circ\iota_{k^{\prime}}
is homotopic to the constant map for
k\neq k^{\prime}
.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}

Here
\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}
is defined to be a
\mathbb{Z}\pi
-module via the map
p_{k}
. Our goal is to show that the composition of the two bottom horizontal maps has kernel needing at least
t^{\prime}
generators. By our condition above on the maps
\iota_{k}
and
p_{k}
, it will suffice to show that the bottom right horizontal map has nontrivial kernel. The result will then follow since we have
t^{\prime}
distinct
H_{k}
factors in
\pi
.

For this final step, consider the following commutative diagram, where we have used the change of coefficients map corresponding to
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}]}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}]}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}]}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{k})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{k})_{*}}

The composition
B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\iota_{k}}B^{s}\xrightarrow{p_{k}}B^{\prime}
is homotopic to the identity, and hence
(p_{k})_{*}\circ(\iota_{k})_{*}=\operatorname{Id}
. It follows that the top left vertical map is injective. By
Corollary

7.6
, the kernel of the top right horizontal map has a
\mathbb{Z}/2
subgroup, call it
K
, which is mapped nontrivially to
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}]}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}])
, and onward by the vertical injection
(\iota_{k})_{*}
to
\mathbb{F}_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}]}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{F}_{2}[\pi^{\prime}])
. By the commutativity of the diagram,
K
is mapped nontrivially to
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})
under the middle vertical
(\iota_{k})_{*}
. By definition,
K
maps trivially to
H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
, and so maps trivially to
H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
in the bottom right. Hence by the commutativity of the diagram, the image of
K
in
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})
, which we know is nontrivial, maps trivially to
H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
In other words, the map
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime}}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\prime})\to H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
has nontrivial kernel, as desired.
∎

The following corollary shows that
Theorem

2.4
(
3
) holds for
4
-manifolds with fundamental group
\pi
as in (
7.1
). We will also use it in the proof of
Proposition

8.22
, showing that for a
3
-manifold group
\pi
and homomorphism
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
such that
(\pi,w)
is admissible, the pair
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
as in (
7.1
) and orientation character
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
such that
(\pi,w)
is admissible.
Let
t^{\prime}\leq t
be such that, for some identification as in (
7.1
), the image of the fundamental class
[M]
in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\bigoplus_{k=1}^{t}H_{4}(H_{k};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{t}
is trivial in the first
t^{\prime}
summands and nontrivial for
k>t^{\prime}
.
Let
B:=P_{2}(M)
. Then the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
equals the kernel of
\varphi_{B}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.

Proof.

By the commutativity of the diagram in (
2.7
), we know that
\ev^{*}\circ\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon=\Theta_{B}\circ\varphi_{B}
. Since
\pi
is a
3
-manifold group, by
Proposition

5.9

\ev^{*}
is an isomorphism. Therefore, the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
is contained in the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
.

By
Proposition

7.2
, the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
is a subgroup of
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{t^{\prime}}
, where the image of the fundamental class
[M]
in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\bigoplus_{k=1}^{t}H_{4}(H_{k};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{t}
is trivial in the first
t^{\prime}
summands and nontrivial for
k>t^{\prime}
. On the other hand, the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
needs at least
t^{\prime}
generators by
Lemma

7.9
. Therefore the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
equals the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
, as needed.
∎

8.
Property 4HL

In this section we discuss the
4
th homology lifting property
, henceforth known as Property 4HL. This will help us to show that
Theorem

2.4
(
2
) holds. More precisely, it seeks to establish criteria for deciding whether an element in the codomain of
\varphi_{X}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
lies in the image, for an arbitrary CW complex
X
with
\pi_{1}(X)=\pi
.
We will first define a version for
3
-coconnected CW complexes and homomorphisms
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
, and later we will define a version for groups. We will end this section by showing that
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL, where
\pi
is a
3
-manifold group and
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
is a homomorphism, such that
(\pi,w)
is admissible (
Proposition

8.22
).

8.1.
Property 4HL for CW complexes

Definition 8.1

(Property 4HL)
.

Let
X
be a connected
CW
complex and write
\pi:=\pi_{1}(X)
. Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism.
We say that
(X,w)
has
Property 4HL
if for every 2-connected map
c\colon X\to B\pi
and for every

x\in\ker(c_{*}\colon H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))

such that

\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0\in\mathbb{Z}\pi

for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, we have that
x
lies in the image of
\varphi_{X}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\xrightarrow{}H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.

Remark 8.2
.

Different choices of 2-connected maps
c\colon X\to B\pi
are related by automorphisms of
\pi
, and therefore they determine the same kernel
K:=\ker(H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))
. So to verify that Property 4HL holds it suffices to fix one choice of
c
.

We also note that the image of
\varphi_{X}
equals the image of the map
H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
, so we could have equivalently asked for
x
to lie in the image of the latter map in the definition of Property 4HL.

The next lemma shows that Property 4HL is independent of stabilisations for
3
-coconnected CW complexes.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
B
be a connected,
3
-coconnected
CW
complex and let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(B)
. Let
c_{B}\colon B\to B\pi
be a 2-connected map. Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Let
B^{s}:=P_{2}(B\vee S^{2})
. Then
(B,w)
has Property 4HL if and only if
(B^{s},w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

First we need some setup. Let
K:=\ker(H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))
. We consider the Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration
\widetilde{B}\to B\xrightarrow{c_{B}}B\pi
, where
\widetilde{B}
is the universal cover, converging to
H_{*}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Note that
H_{1}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})=0
. In addition, since
B
is
3
-coconnected, we know
\widetilde{B}\simeq K(\pi_{2}(B),2)
, and so
\pi_{3}(\widetilde{B})=0
. By the Hurewicz theorem, the Hurewicz map
\pi_{3}(\widetilde{B})\to H_{3}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
is surjective, and hence
H_{3}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})=0
. Note that
K
is the term
F_{2,2}
in the filtration of
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
given by convergence of the spectral sequence and there is an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})\to K\to E\to 0

with
E:=E_{2,2}^{\infty}
a quotient of
H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w})
. Here we used that
\pi_{2}(B)\cong H_{2}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
.
Similarly, for
K^{s}:=\ker(H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))
we have an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to K^{s}\to E^{s}\to 0

with
E^{s}
the
E^{\infty}_{2,2}
term of the corresponding Leray–Serre spectral sequence for
\widetilde{B}^{s}\to B^{s}\xrightarrow{c_{B^{s}}}B\pi
. Again
E^{s}
is a quotient of
H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B^{s})^{w})
. The isomorphism
H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B^{s})^{w})\cong H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w})
induces an isomorphism
E^{s}\xrightarrow{\cong}E
. To see this use the definition of
E^{s}
and
E
as the quotients of
H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B^{s})^{w})\cong H_{2}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w})
by the image of the
d_{3}
map on
H_{4}(B\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Then naturality of the spectral sequences shows that the quotients agree.

Consider the inclusion and collapse maps
B\to B\vee S^{2}\to B
. Passing to the Postnikov
2
-types yields maps
B\xrightarrow{\iota}B^{s}\xrightarrow{\rho}B
.
Then, comparing the spectral sequences, using
\iota
and
\rho
, we obtain the following induced commutative diagram with exact rows.

(8.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Now we are ready to prove that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL if and only if
(B^{s},w)
has Property 4HL. First suppose that
(B^{s},w)
has Property 4HL. We will show that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL. So let
x\in K
and assume that
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0\in\mathbb{Z}\pi
for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Define
x^{s}:=\iota_{*}(x)\in H_{4}(B^{s},\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Then
x^{s}
lies in
K^{s}
, because
c_{B}\colon B\to B\pi
factors through
\iota
. Moreover for all
\alpha^{s},\beta^{s}\in H^{2}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
we have, in
\mathbb{Z}\pi
, that

\langle\alpha^{s},\beta^{s}\cap x^{s}\rangle=\langle\alpha^{s},\beta^{s}\cap\iota_{*}(x)\rangle=\langle\alpha^{s},\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\beta^{s})\cap x)\rangle=\langle\iota^{*}(\alpha^{s}),\iota^{*}(\beta^{s})\cap x\rangle=0.

Then, since
(B^{s},w)
has Property 4HL, we know that
x^{s}\in K^{s}
lies in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. So
x^{s}
has trivial image in
E^{s}
. By the top right square of (
8.4
),
x
has trivial image in
E
, and so lies in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Thus
(B,w)
has Property 4HL.

It remains to show that Property 4HL for
(B,w)
implies Property 4HL for
(B^{s},w)
. We will apply essentially the same argument as above, but now using the bottom two rows of (
8.4
). Assume that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL. Let
x^{s}\in K^{s}
such that
\langle\alpha^{s},\beta^{s}\cap x^{s}\rangle=0\in\mathbb{Z}\pi
for all
\alpha^{s},\beta^{s}\in H^{2}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Define
x:=\rho_{*}(x^{s})\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Then
x\in K
since the map
c_{B^{s}}
factors through
\rho
. We also have

\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=\langle\alpha,\beta\cap\rho_{*}(x^{s})\rangle=\langle\alpha,\rho_{*}(\rho^{*}(\beta)\cap x^{s})\rangle=\langle\rho^{*}(\alpha),\rho^{*}(\beta)\cap x^{s}\rangle=0,

for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. So by Property 4HL for
(B,w)
, the element
x
lies in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, and thus has trivial image in
E
. By the bottom right square of (
8.4
), the element
x^{s}\in K^{s}
also has trivial image in
E^{s}
, and hence lies in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B^{s};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Thus Property 4HL for
(B,w)
implies Property 4HL for
(B^{s},w)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

For
i=1,\dots,n
, let
B_{i}
be a connected,
3
-coconnected CW complex with fundamental group
G_{i}
. Let
X:=\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}B_{i}
and let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(X)\cong\ast_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}
. Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. If each
(B_{i},w|_{G_{i}})
has Property 4HL, for
i=1,\dots,n
, then
(X,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

We will write
w_{i}
for
w|_{G_{i}}
to make the proof more readable.
Let

K_{i}:=\ker(H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w_{i}})\to H_{4}(G_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w_{i}}))\text{ and }K:=\ker(H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})).

Using the Mayer–Vietoris sequence, we have that
K\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}K_{i}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z})\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}^{w_{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}G_{i}}H_{4}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z})
, where
\widetilde{X}
and
\widetilde{B_{i}}
are the universal covers. For each
x\in K
with
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0\in\mathbb{Z}\pi
for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, write
x_{i}
for the image of
x
in
K_{i}
. We can view
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\in H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
as elements of
H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, again using a Mayer–Vietoris sequence. Then

\langle\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\cap x_{i}\rangle=\langle\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\cap x_{i}\rangle+\sum_{j\neq i}\langle 0,0\cap x_{j}\rangle=\langle\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\cap x\rangle=0.

By assumption
(B_{i},{w_{i}})
has Property 4HL and hence for each
i
there exists a preimage
y_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}G_{i}}H_{4}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z})
of
x_{i}\in K_{i}
. By naturality of the Mayer–Vietoris sequence, taking
\sum_{i}y_{i}\in\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}K_{i}\cong K
we have that
\varphi_{X}(\sum_{i}y_{i})=x\in H_{4}(X;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
Hence
(X,w)
has Property 4HL.
∎

We will need the following lemma on
3
-coconnected CW complexes later in this section to establish Property 4HL for
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
, as well as in the proof of
Corollary

10.2
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
B
be a connected,
3
-coconnected CW complex with fundamental group
\pi:=\pi_{1}(B)
and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Assume that
\pi_{2}(B)
is projective as a
\mathbb{Z}\pi
-module. Then there is an exact sequence

H_{5}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to 0,

where the third map is induced by some classifying map
B\to B\pi
inducing an isomorphism on fundamental groups.

Proof.

Since
\pi_{2}(B)
is projective, there is a decomposition
\pi_{2}(B)\oplus Q\cong\mathbb{Z}\pi^{m}
for some
\mathbb{Z}\pi
-module
Q
and for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
, and hence

H_{n}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w})\leq H_{n}(\pi;\pi_{2}(B)^{w})\oplus H_{n}(\pi;Q^{w})\cong H_{n}(\pi;(\pi_{2}(B)\oplus Q)^{w})\cong H_{n}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}\pi^{m})=0

for all
n>0
. Furthermore,
\pi_{2}(B)
is free as an abelian group, and therefore projective. Thus for the universal cover
\widetilde{B}\simeq K(\pi_{2}(B),2)\simeq\prod\mathbb{CP}^{\infty}
we have
H_{2k+1}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H_{2k+1}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})=0
for all
k
.

Consider the Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration
\widetilde{B}\to B\to B\pi
. On the
4
-line the only nontrivial terms are
H_{0}(\pi;H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
and
H_{4}(B\pi;H_{0}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})^{w})\cong H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
The codomains of all differentials going out of
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
are trivial, while the only possibly nontrivial differential into
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
is
d_{5}\colon H_{5}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
.

Hence
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
fits into an extension

0\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)/\operatorname{Im}d_{5}\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to 0

and the lemma follows.
∎

8.2.
Property 4HL for groups

Definition 8.7
.

Let
\pi
be a finitely presented group and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. We say that
(\pi,w)
has
Property 4HL
if the pair
(P_{2}(M),w)
has Property 4HL, for every closed
4
-manifold
M
with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
\pi
be a finite group and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism.
Then
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
. Let
B:=P_{2}(M)
.
Let
x\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
be such that
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0
for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B,\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. We will show that
x
lies in the image of the map
\varphi_{B}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
We will use the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{*}}

analogous to (
2.7
). Recall that we know
\ev^{*}
is injective by
Corollary

5.2
.
Since
\pi
is finite, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
kx=\varphi_{B}(y)
– see for example
[
45
]
*(3.3). By
Proposition

6.15
,
y
is in the torsion subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
since its image in
\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
is zero and
\ev^{*}
is injective. As a result,
kx
, and therefore also
x
, is in the torsion subgroup of
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Teichner
[
67
]
showed that the torsion subgroup of
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
lies in the image of
\varphi_{B}
for every finite group (see also
[
45
]
*Proof of Theorem 3.4). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
\pi=\mathbb{Z}
and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Then
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi=\mathbb{Z}
and orientation character
w
. Let
B:=P_{2}(M)
. By
Lemma

3.6
, we know that
\pi_{2}(M)\cong\pi_{2}(B)
is stably free and therefore projective. So we can apply
Lemma

8.6
to yield the exact sequence

H_{5}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to 0.

Since
\pi
is geometrically 1-dimensional, it follows that the map
\varphi_{B}
is an isomorphism. This implies that
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.
∎

8.3.
Property 4HL for
PD_{3}
-groups

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
\pi
be a
PD_{3}
-group and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Then
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

Let
X
be an aspherical
PD_{3}
-complex with fundamental group
\pi
, orientation character
v^{\prime}\colon\pi\to C_{2}
, and a single top cell. Then
K:=X^{(2)}
has
H_{2}(K;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong\mathbb{Z}\pi
and
H^{2}(K;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong I\pi^{v^{\prime}}
. Let
v:=wv^{\prime}
. By
[
43
]
*Lemma 7.12, there is a
4
-manifold
N
with fundamental group
\pi
, orientation character
w
, second homotopy group
\pi_{2}(N)\cong I\pi^{v}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi
, and, under the decomposition

\Her^{w}(I\pi^{v}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong\Her^{w}(I\pi^{v})\oplus\Her^{w}(\mathbb{Z}\pi)\oplus\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{v},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger})\cong\Her^{w}(I\pi)\oplus\Her^{w}(\mathbb{Z}\pi)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi,

where the second isomorphism uses
[
37
]
*Lemma 7.5, the intersection form
\lambda_{N}
maps to
(0,*,1)
.

By
[
51
]
(see also
[
44
]
*Theorem 1.2, Section 1.5), and since
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})=0
, every other
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
is homeomorphic to
N
modulo connected sum with copies of
\mathbb{CP}^{2},\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
, and
\star\mathbb{CP}^{2}
. Using
Lemma

8.3
, it suffices to show that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL for
B:=P_{2}(N)
.

The Leray–Serre spectral sequence for the fibration
\widetilde{B}\to B\to B\pi
yields the short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\xrightarrow{q}\mathbb{Z}\to 0.

Let
y\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
be a class such that for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
we have
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap y\rangle=0
, i.e.
y\in\ker\Theta_{B}
. Since
\pi
is a
PD_{3}
-group we have that
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})=0
so there is no corresponding condition on
y
.
Let
k:=q(y)
be the image of
y
in
\mathbb{Z}
.
We then have to show that
k
is zero, so that
y
lies in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
. It will follow that
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

By comparing the above Leray–Serre spectral sequence for
\widetilde{B}\to B\to B\pi
with the corresponding Leray–Serre spectral sequence for
\widetilde{N}\to N\to B\pi
, via a
3
-connected map
f\colon N\to B
, we obtain a commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(N;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Therefore,
f_{*}[N]\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
maps to
\pm 1\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus
kf_{*}[N]\mp y
is in the image of
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
.
We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{*}}

again following (
2.7
).
Since
y\in\ker\Theta_{B}
we have that
\Theta_{B}(kf_{*}[N]\pm y)=kf_{*}\lambda_{N}
. Let
\lambda:=f_{*}\lambda_{N}
. Then we have that
k\lambda
lies in the image of
\Theta_{B}\circ\varphi_{B}
. By the diagram,
k\lambda
lies in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\,\,\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
. Since
\Upsilon
is an isomorphism this implies
k\lambda
lies in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\,\,\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
.

Under the decomposition

\Her^{w}(I\pi^{v}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong\Her^{w}(I\pi^{v})\oplus\Her^{w}(\mathbb{Z}\pi)\oplus\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{v},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger})\cong\Her^{w}(I\pi^{v})\oplus\Her^{w}(\mathbb{Z}\pi)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\pi,

where the second isomorphism uses
[
37
]
*Lemma 7.5, the element
k\lambda
maps to
(0,*,k)
using the description of
\lambda
at the start of the proof.
By
Lemma

6.10
, the component of the image of
k\lambda
in
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{v},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger})\cong\mathbb{Z}\pi
lies in the image of
I\pi^{v}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\mathbb{Z}\pi\to\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{v},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger})
. Under the isomorphisms
I\pi^{v}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\mathbb{Z}\pi\cong I\pi^{v}
and
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{v},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger})\cong\mathbb{Z}\pi
, this map corresponds to the inclusion
I\pi^{v}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\pi
. In particular,
k
lies in the image only for
k=0
. Since above we had that
kf_{*}[N]\mp y
lies in the image of
\varphi_{B}
, and we now know that
k=0
, we deduce that
y
lies in the image of
\varphi_{B}
. Hence
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.
∎

8.4.
Property 4HL for
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2

In this subsection let
\pi=\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2=\langle t,T\mid[T,t],T^{2}\rangle
and let
v^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Z}\pi\to C_{2}
be given by
v^{\prime}(t)=1
and
v^{\prime}(T)=-1
. Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be such that
w(T)=1
. We will show in
Lemma

8.13
that
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL, but we will need a
couple of preliminary lemmas.
Let
v=wv^{\prime}
. We will need to consider both the untwisted augmentation ideal
I\pi
, as well as the twisted augmentation ideals
I\pi^{w}
,
I\pi^{v^{\prime}}
, and
I\pi^{v}
.

Lemma 8.11
.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
such that
w(T)=1
. Let
B
be a connected,
3
-coconnected CW complex, and let
f\colon M\to B
be
3
-connected.
Then there is an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to\mathbb{Z}/2\to 0,

and the image of
f_{*}[M]
in
\mathbb{Z}/2
is nontrivial.

Proof.

If
\pi_{2}(M)\cong\pi_{2}(B)
is stably free as a
\mathbb{Z}\pi
-module, then there is an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{4}(B^{w};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to 0

by
Lemma

8.6
. By
Lemma

3.9
,
\pi_{2}(M)
is stably free if and only if the image of
[M]
in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
is nontrivial. This completes the proof of the lemma in this case.

If
\pi_{2}(M)
is not stably free, then it is stably isomorphic to
I\pi\oplus I\pi^{v}
by
Lemma

3.9
, where
v=wv^{\prime}
. We now prove the lemma in this case. Let
\widetilde{M}
denote the universal cover of
M
. Note that
H_{3}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\cong H^{1}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
and
H_{3}(\widetilde{M};\mathbb{Z})\cong H_{3}(M;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Z}\pi^{w})\cong H^{1}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}\pi^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{w}
, where the last isomorphism follows from the fact that
\pi
has two ends
[
20
]
*Theorem 13.5.5. Consider the spectral sequence with
E^{2}
term
H_{p}(\pi;H_{q}(\widetilde{M};\mathbb{Z})^{w})
converging to
H_{p+q}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. On the
3
-line of the
E^{2}
page we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{3}(\widetilde{M};\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\cong\mathbb{Z};

\displaystyle H_{1}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\cong H_{1}(\pi;I\pi^{w})\oplus H_{1}(\pi;I\pi^{v^{\prime}})\cong H_{2}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\oplus H_{2}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{v^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2;

\displaystyle H_{3}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{Z}/2.

On the
E^{\infty}
page, these terms will produce the associated graded group for the cyclic group
H_{3}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Since only cyclic groups can arise in such an associated graded group, it follows that the
d_{3}
-differential
\mathbb{Z}/2\cong H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{1}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})
is nontrivial and thus injective.

On the
4
-line of the
E^{2}
page we have

\displaystyle H_{1}(\pi;H_{3}(\widetilde{M};\mathbb{Z})^{w})

\displaystyle\cong H_{1}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2;

\displaystyle H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\cong H_{3}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\oplus H_{3}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{v^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2;

\displaystyle H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{Z}/2.

We already saw that the
d_{3}
map out of
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
is injective, so that term does not survive to the
E^{\infty}
page. The
d_{2}
-differential
H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2\to H_{0}(\pi;H_{3}(\widetilde{M};\mathbb{Z})^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}
is trivial, so
H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})
survives to the
E^{3}
page. Since
H_{4}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
is again cyclic, the
d_{3}
-differential
H_{5}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\to H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2
is nontrivial and therefore injective. Hence from the
E^{\infty}
page we obtain an extension
\mathbb{Z}\to H_{4}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})/\operatorname{Im}d_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\to 0
.

Now we consider the analogous spectral sequence with
E^{2}
term
H_{p}(\pi;H_{q}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})^{w})
converging to
H_{p+q}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
, where
\widetilde{B}
is the universal cover of
B
. In this case the
d_{2}
-differential
H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\oplus\mathbb{Z}/2\to H_{0}(\pi;H_{3}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})^{w})=0
is necessarily trivial. Since
f\colon M\to B
is
3
-connected, the same
d_{3}
-differentials as above are nontrivial, and we have the following commutative diagram with exact rows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}/2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}/2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Since
[M]
is a generator of
H_{4}(M;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
it follows that the image of
f_{*}[M]
is nontrivial in
H_{2}(\pi;I\pi^{w}\oplus I\pi^{v^{\prime}})/\operatorname{Im}d_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
as claimed.
∎

Lemma 8.12
.

The map
e\colon I\pi\to I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}
sending
x\mapsto(f\mapsto f(x))
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The statement of the lemma is independent of
w
, but
w
appears indirectly in the proof, in the involution used to consider
I\pi^{\dagger}
as a left module. For simplicity, we assume that
w
is trivial.

By
[
43
]
*Lemma 7.14, the map
\theta\colon I\pi^{v^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\cong}I\pi^{\dagger}
sending

\displaystyle 1-t

\displaystyle\mapsto\Bigg\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1-t&\mapsto&1-t\\
1-T&\mapsto&1-T\end{array}\Bigg\}\text{ and }1+T\mapsto\Bigg\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1-t&\mapsto&1+T\\
1-T&\mapsto&0\end{array}\Bigg\}

is an isomorphism. Similarly one sees that the map
\theta^{\prime}\colon I\pi\xrightarrow{\cong}(I\pi^{v^{\prime}})^{\dagger}
sending

\displaystyle 1-t\mapsto\Bigg\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1-t&\mapsto&1-t\\
1+T&\mapsto&1+T\end{array}\Bigg\}\text{ and }1-T\mapsto\Bigg\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}1-t&\mapsto&1-T\\
1+T&\mapsto&0\end{array}\Bigg\}

is an isomorphism. By a straightforward computation,

\theta^{\prime}=\theta^{\dagger}\circ e\colon I\pi\to(I\pi^{v^{\prime}})^{\dagger}.

It follows that
e
is an isomorphism as claimed.
∎

Now we are ready to show that the group
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
has Property 4HL with respect to a certain map
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\to C_{2}
.

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
w\colon\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\to C_{2}
be such that
w(T)=1
.
The pair
(\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

Let
\pi:=\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
. Since
w(T)=1
, the orientation character is determined by
w(t)=\pm 1\in C_{2}
.
For both
w(t)=\pm 1
, we have that
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
and thus by
[
51
]
(see also
[
44
]
*Theorem 1.2, Section 1.5) up to connected sum with
\mathbb{CP}^{2},\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
and
\star\mathbb{CP}^{2}
there are only two homeomorphism classes with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
. By
Lemma

8.3
, it suffices to consider one manifold from both classes.

First we consider the case of
w
trivial.
The nontrivial class in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
is represented by
M=S^{1}\times\mathbb{RP}^{3}
, and the trivial class by a
4
-manifold
N
obtained as the boundary of a 5-dimensional thickening of the 2-skeleton of
B\pi
.

Consider the nontrivial class in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})
and
B:=P_{2}(M)
, where
M=S^{1}\times\mathbb{RP}^{2}
. Since
\pi_{2}(B)=0
and so is certainly projective, by
Lemma

8.6
there is an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})\to 0.

In particular, every class in the kernel of
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})
belongs to the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
and thus
(B,w)
has Property 4HL.

Now we consider the trivial class in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})
.
By
[
43
]
*Lemma 7.12, the manifold
N
has
\pi_{1}(N)\cong\pi
, the second homotopy group
\pi_{2}(N)\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}
, and hyperbolic intersection form. Let
P:=P_{2}(N)
. We need to show that
(P,w)
has Property 4HL.

Let
f\colon N\to P
be
3
-connected. By
Lemma

8.11
, there is an exact sequence

(8.14)

\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{P};\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{P}}H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z})\to\mathbb{Z}/2\to 0

and the image of
f_{*}[N]
in
\mathbb{Z}/2
is nontrivial. Let
y\in H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z})
be a class such that
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap y\rangle=0
for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, i.e.
y\in\ker\Theta_{P}
. It follows from the spectral sequence computation in the proof of
Lemma

8.11
that the map
H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z})
is the trivial map, so we must consider all
y\in\ker\Theta_{P}
.
By exactness of (
8.14
), we have to show that the image of
y
in
\mathbb{Z}/2
is trivial. Assume for a contradiction that
y
maps nontrivially to
\mathbb{Z}/2
. Then
f_{*}[N]-y
maps to
0\in\mathbb{Z}/2
and hence lies in the image of
\varphi_{P}\colon\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{P};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z})
.
We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(P;\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her(H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her(H^{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{*}}

We have that
\Theta_{P}(f_{*}[N]-y)=f_{*}\lambda_{N}
. Let
\lambda:=f_{*}\lambda_{N}
. Then
\Theta_{P}(f_{*}[N]-y)=\lambda
, and so we deduce that
\lambda
lies in the image of
\Theta_{P}\circ\varphi_{P}
.
By the diagram, it follows that
\lambda
lies in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\,\,\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}\circ\Upsilon
, or equivalently, since
\Upsilon
is an isomorphism, that
\lambda
lies in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\,\,\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
.

Since
P=P_{2}(N)
, we have that
H^{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H^{2}(N;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong H_{2}(N;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}
.
Under the decomposition
\Her(I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger})\cong\Her(I\pi)\oplus\Her(I\pi^{\dagger})\oplus\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi,I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})
, the element
\lambda
maps to
(0,0,e)
, where
e\colon I\pi\to I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}
is as in
Lemma

8.12
, since
\lambda
is hyperbolic.

We will obtain a contradiction by showing that
\lambda
is not in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\,\,\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
. The desired contradiction will prove that the image of
y
in
\mathbb{Z}/2
is trivial after all, and hence by exactness of (
8.14
) that
y
lies in the image of
\varphi_{P}
.

We examine the pre-image of
\lambda
under
\ev^{*}
.
Note that
(I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger})^{\dagger}\cong I\pi^{\dagger}\oplus I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}
. The bottom left group of Hermitian forms
\Her(H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})
has a similar decomposition as in
Remark

6.9
, with

\Her(I\pi^{\dagger}\oplus I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})\cong\Her(I\pi^{\dagger})\oplus\Her(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})\oplus\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}).

The map
\ev\colon H^{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger}
translates to a map
\ev\colon I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}\to I\pi^{\dagger}\oplus I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}
, given by
(x,g)\mapsto(g,e(x))
.
The coordinates are switched because we used Poincaré duality in the identification
H^{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}
. A straightforward check shows that
e
lies in the image of
\ev^{*}
, and that

\ev^{*}(\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}})=e
.
That is, one has to check that,
(0,0,\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}})
induces, under the map
\ev
, the form corresponding to
(0,0,e)
.
Moreover
\ev^{*}
is injective by
Proposition

5.9
, so
(0,0,\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}})
represents the unique pre-image of
\lambda
in
\Her(H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})
. So we have to show that
(0,0,\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}})
is not in the image of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
.

Now we consider the map
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
. The compatibility of the decomposition in
Lemma

6.8
and the decomposition from
Remark

6.9
with respect to
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
, as in
Lemma

6.10
, means it suffices to show that
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}}
does not lie in the image of the map

I\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}\to\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})

from
Lemma

6.10
.

The map
e\colon I\pi\to I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}
is an isomorphism by
Lemma

8.12
. Using this we have an isomorphism
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi,I\pi)
sending
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}}
to
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi}
, and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},\mathbb{Z}\pi^{\dagger\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi,I\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi,\mathbb{Z}\pi).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\dagger\dagger}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}

The top vertical maps are those induced by
\mathcal{B}_{I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{Z}\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}}
, described in
Lemma

6.10
. The lower vertical maps are induced by
e
. The horizontal maps are induced by the inclusion
j\colon I\pi\to\mathbb{Z}\pi
. We want to see that
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi}
is not in the image of the left vertical composition.
Under the lower horizontal map,
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi}
maps to the inclusion
j\colon I\pi\to\mathbb{Z}\pi
, which lifts to
1\otimes j
in the top right
\mathbb{Z}\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi
.
Thus if we show that
1\otimes j
is not in the image of the top horizontal map
I\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}\to\mathbb{Z}\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}
, it follows from the diagram that
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi}
is not in the image of the left vertical composition, and hence that
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}}
is not in the image of the top left vertical map, as desired.

Recall that
v^{\prime}\colon\pi\to C_{2}
is given by
v^{\prime}(t)=1
and
v^{\prime}(T)=-1
.
Using the group presentation
\pi\cong\langle t,T\mid[t,T],T^{2}\rangle
, there is an isomorphism
\theta^{-1}\colon I\pi^{\dagger}\to I\pi^{v^{\prime}}
sending
j
to
(1-t)
; see the proof of
Lemma

8.12
.
The composition

I\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{j\otimes\operatorname{Id}}\mathbb{Z}\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\otimes\theta^{-1}}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{v^{\prime}}

is the zero homomorphism, where
\varepsilon\colon\mathbb{Z}\pi\to\mathbb{Z}
is the augmentation, and
(\varepsilon\otimes\theta^{-1})(1\otimes j)=1\otimes(1-t)
. Thus if
1\otimes j
is in the image of
j\otimes\operatorname{Id}
, it follows that
1\otimes(1-t)=0\in\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{v^{\prime}}
. It therefore suffices to show that
1\otimes(1-t)
is nontrivial. For this we consider the exact sequence

0\to\Tor_{1}^{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}^{v^{\prime}})\to\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{v^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\mathbb{Z}\pi\to\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\mathbb{Z}^{v^{\prime}}\to 0.

This can be identified with the exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}/2\to\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{v^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}/2\to 0

where
1\otimes(1-t)
maps to
0\in\mathbb{Z}
and
1\otimes(1+T)
maps to
2\in\mathbb{Z}
. Since
1\otimes(1-t)
and
1\otimes(1+T)
generate
\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{v^{\prime}}
, it follows that
1\in\mathbb{Z}/2
maps to
1\otimes(1-t)
. In particular, the latter is nontrivial as needed.

Recapping, this means that
1\otimes j
is not in the image of
j\otimes\operatorname{Id}
, which implies that
\operatorname{Id}_{I\pi^{\dagger\dagger}}
is not in the image of the restriction of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
to the map
I\pi\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}I\pi^{\dagger}\to\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(I\pi^{\dagger\dagger},I\pi^{\dagger\dagger})
that is the top left vertical map of the previous diagram. This in turn implies that
\lambda=f_{*}\lambda_{N}
is not in the image of
\ev^{*}\circ\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
, which as explained above leads to the desired contradiction. So
y
maps to
0\in\mathbb{Z}/2
, and therefore
y
lies in the image of
\varphi_{B}
, as required. Thus
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL when
w
is trivial.

The case where
w(t)=-1
is similar. Here one has to consider the mapping torus
M^{\prime}
of the orientation reversing involution on
\mathbb{RP}^{3}
in place of
S^{1}\times\mathbb{RP}^{3}
, representing the nontrivial element of
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
. Since
\pi_{2}(M^{\prime})=0
, letting
B^{\prime}:=P_{2}(M^{\prime})
, we again have an exact sequence

\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\widetilde{B}^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B^{\prime}}}H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to 0

by
Lemma

8.6
. Again, this implies that every element in the kernel of
H_{4}(B^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
lies in the image of
\varphi_{B^{\prime}}
.

For
0\in H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
there is a
4
-manifold
N^{\prime}
with
\pi_{2}(N^{\prime})\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{wv^{\prime}}\cong I\pi\oplus I\pi^{\dagger}
and hyperbolic intersection form. Here we use that the involution of
\mathbb{Z}\pi
in this case is given by
g\mapsto w(g)g^{-1}
so that as a left module
I\pi^{\dagger}
is isomorphic to
I\pi^{wv^{\prime}}
.
Let
P^{\prime}:=P_{2}(N^{\prime})
. We just observed that
H_{2}(P^{\prime};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
is isomorphic to
H_{2}(P;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. We also have the exact sequence from
Lemma

8.11
for
w(t)=-1
. The analysis above applies unchanged, to show that for
y\in\ker\Theta_{P^{\prime}}
we have that
y\mapsto 0\in\mathbb{Z}/2
, and hence
y\in\operatorname{Im}\varphi_{P^{\prime}}
. This shows that
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL for
w(T)=1
and
w(t)=-1
.
∎

8.5.
Property 4HL for
3
-manifold groups that are nontrivial free products

In this section we establish Property 4HL for all admissible
(\pi,w)
,
deducing it for
3
-manifold groups that are nontrivial free products of factors for which we already proved Property 4HL in
Sections

8.2
,
8.3
, and
8.4
.

We work initially with an arbitrary finitely presented group
\pi
, a subgroup
G\leq\pi
, and a character
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
. Only at the end of the section, in
Proposition

8.22
, do we restrict to admissible
3
-manifold groups and orientation characters. For
G\leq\pi
, we also prove results for an arbitrary left
\mathbb{Z}G
-module
A
. Recall that
\Sigma_{2}
denotes the symmetric group on two elements.

Definition 8.15
.

Let
G\leq\pi
be a subgroup. Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism and define the composition
\overline{w}\colon G\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
. For every
\mathbb{Z}G
-module
A
we define

\displaystyle\psi\colon\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A)

\displaystyle\to(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)

\displaystyle\gamma\otimes(a\otimes a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\gamma\otimes a)\otimes(\gamma\otimes a^{\prime})

and

\displaystyle\xi\colon\Sesq^{\overline{w}}(A)

\displaystyle\to\Sesq^{w}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((\gamma\otimes a,\gamma^{\prime}\otimes a^{\prime})\mapsto\gamma\lambda(a,a^{\prime})\overline{\gamma^{\prime}}\big).

In the case that
A
is free as an abelian group, recall that
\Gamma(A)
is isomorphic to the group of fixed points of
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A
under the
\Sigma_{2}
-action permuting the two copies of
A
. Therefore, taking fixed points, we obtain

\displaystyle\psi^{\Sigma_{2}}\colon\Ind_{G}^{\pi}\Gamma(A)

\displaystyle\to\Gamma(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)\text{ and}

\displaystyle\xi^{\Sigma_{2}}\colon\Her^{\overline{w}}(A)

\displaystyle\to\Her^{w}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A).

Recall that for
G\leq\pi
, and a
\mathbb{Z}G
-module
A
, we denote the module
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}G}(A,\mathbb{Z}G)
by
A^{\star}
.

Lemma 8.16
.

Let
G\leq\pi
be a subgroup, let
A
be a
\mathbb{Z}G
-module, and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Define the composition
\overline{w}\colon G\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
. Then we have the commutative diagram

(8.17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sesq^{\overline{w}}(A^{\star})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sesq^{w}((\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)^{\dagger}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}

where

(1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(\gamma\otimes(a\otimes a^{\prime}))=\big((f,f^{\prime})\mapsto w(\gamma)\overline{f(a)}f(a^{\prime})\big)
, and

(2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A}((\gamma\otimes a)\otimes(\gamma^{\prime}\otimes a^{\prime}))=\big((h,h^{\prime})\mapsto\overline{h(\gamma\otimes a)}h^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}\otimes a^{\prime})\big)
,

and the maps
\psi
and
\xi
are from
Definition

8.15
.

Here we used the isomorphism
\Sesq^{w}((\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)^{\dagger})\cong\Sesq^{w}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A^{\star}))
induced by the isomorphism from
Lemma

4.8
, in order to use the map
\xi
.

Proof.

We have

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}(\psi(\gamma\otimes(a\otimes a^{\prime})))=\big((h,h^{\prime})\mapsto\overline{h(\gamma\otimes a)}h^{\prime}(\gamma\otimes a^{\prime})\big).

Applying the isomorphism from
Lemma

4.8
, we obtain the element of
\Sesq^{w}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A^{\star}))
given by

(\delta\otimes f,\delta^{\prime}\otimes f^{\prime})\mapsto(\overline{\gamma f(a)\overline{\delta}})(\gamma f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})\overline{\delta^{\prime}})=w(\gamma)\delta\overline{f(a)}f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})\overline{\delta^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, we also see

\xi(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{A}(\gamma\otimes(a\otimes a^{\prime})))=\xi\big((h,h^{\prime})\mapsto w(\gamma)\overline{h(a)}h^{\prime}(a^{\prime})\big)=\big((\delta\otimes f,\delta^{\prime}\otimes f^{\prime})\mapsto w(\gamma)\delta\overline{f(a)}f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})\overline{\delta^{\prime}}\big)

as well.
Hence the diagram commutes as claimed.
∎

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
G\leq\pi
be a subgroup, let
A
be a
\mathbb{Z}G
-module, and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism. Define the composition
\overline{w}\colon G\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
. Consider the diagram (
8.17
). Let
x\in\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}(x)
is in the image of
\xi
. Then there exists
b\in\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A)
such that
x-\psi(b)
is in the kernel of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}
.

Furthermore, if
x
is a
\Sigma_{2}
-fixed point, then
b
can be chosen to be a
\Sigma_{2}
-fixed point.

Proof.

Let
x=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(g_{i}\otimes a_{i})\otimes(g_{i}^{\prime}\otimes a_{i}^{\prime})
. Using the isomorphism

\Sesq^{w}((\Ind_{G}^{\pi}A)^{\dagger})\cong\Sesq^{w}(\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A^{\star}))

coming from
Lemma

4.8
, we have that

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}}(A)(x)=((\gamma\otimes f,\gamma^{\prime}\otimes f^{\prime})\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma\overline{f(m_{i})g_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}f^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{i})\overline{\gamma^{\prime}}).

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}(x)
is in the image of
\xi
, there exists a
\lambda\in\Sesq^{w}(A^{\star})
such that for all
f,f^{\prime}\in A^{\star}
we have

\lambda(f,f^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{f(m_{i})g_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}f^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{i}).

Up to reordering, we can assume that there exists an
m
such that
g_{i}^{-1}g_{i}^{\prime}\in G
for
i\leq m
and
g_{i}^{-1}g_{i}^{\prime}\notin G
for
i>m
.
Note that
\overline{f(m_{i})g_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}f^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}[G]
if and only if
g_{i}^{-1}g_{i}^{\prime}\in G
. Since
\lambda(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}[G]
, it follows that

\lambda(f,f^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\overline{f(m_{i})g_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}f^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{i}),

and that
\sum_{i=m+1}^{n}(g_{i}\otimes a_{i})\otimes(g_{i}^{\prime}\otimes a_{i}^{\prime})
is in the kernel of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}
.

By assumption, for each
i\leq m
there exists
h_{i}\in G
such that
g_{i}^{\prime}=g_{i}h^{-1}
. Then
(g_{i}\otimes m_{i})\otimes(g_{i}^{\prime}\otimes m_{i}^{\prime})=(g_{i}\otimes a_{i})\otimes(g_{i}\otimes ha_{i}^{\prime})
and hence

\sum_{i=1}^{m}(g_{i}\otimes a_{i})\otimes(g_{i}^{\prime}\otimes a_{i}^{\prime})=\psi(\sum_{i}g_{i}\otimes(a_{i}\otimes ha_{i}^{\prime})).

Thus setting
b:=\sum_{i}g_{i}\otimes(a_{i}\otimes ha_{i}^{\prime})
we have that
x-\psi(b)
is in the kernel of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Ind_{G}^{\pi}(A)}
as required.

Since
g_{i}^{-1}g_{i}^{\prime}\in G
if and only if
(g_{i}^{\prime})^{-1}g_{i}\in G
, the element
b
defined above is a
\Sigma_{2}
-fixed point if
x
is a
\Sigma_{2}
-fixed point.
∎

Now we consider the case that
\pi\cong*_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}
, where the groups
G_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,n
are finitely presented, and for each
i
we consider left
\mathbb{Z}G_{i}
-modules
A_{i}
.
Let
A:=\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}(A_{i})
and let
s_{i}\colon\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}(A_{i})\to A
denote the canonical inclusion map for each
i
.
Let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism, and for each
i
, consider the composition
\overline{w}_{i}\colon G_{i}\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
.

Lemma 8.19
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(A)
. The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}\Gamma(A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(A_{i}^{\star})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Her^{w}((\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A_{i})^{\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(A^{\dagger})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i}\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i}\mathcal{B}_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i}\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\dagger}}

commutes, where
s
and
s^{\dagger}
are the maps induced by
(\Gamma(s_{i}))_{i}
and
(s_{i}^{\dagger})_{i}
respectively, and each
\psi_{i}
and
\xi_{i}
comes from
Definition

8.15
.
We denote the top composition by
\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}:=s\circ(\oplus_{i}\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}})
and the bottom composition by
\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}}:=s^{\dagger}\circ(\oplus_{i}\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}})
.
If
\mathcal{B}_{A}(x)
lies in the image of
\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}}
, then there exists
b\in\bigoplus\limits_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}\Gamma(A_{i})
such that
x-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(b)\in\ker\mathcal{B}_{A}
.

Proof.

For every
\mathbb{Z}\pi
-module
U
, the map
\mathcal{B}_{U}
is obtained from
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{U}
by taking
\Sigma_{2}
-fixed points and noticing that the result factors through
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(U)
. The left hand square thus commutes by
Lemma

8.16
. The right hand square commutes by naturality of
\mathcal{B}
.

Now assume that
\mathcal{B}_{A}(x)
lies in the image of
\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}}
. Let
y\in\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(A_{i}^{\star})
be a preimage. Then
s^{\dagger}(\oplus_{i}\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(y))=\mathcal{B}_{A}(x)
. By
Lemma

6.10
, there exists
x^{\prime}\in\bigoplus\limits_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A_{i})
with

\oplus_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A}(x^{\prime})=\oplus_{i}\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(y)

such that

x-s(x^{\prime})\in\ker\mathcal{B}_{A}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(A)
. By
Lemma

8.18
, there exists
b\in\bigoplus\limits_{i}\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}(\Gamma(A_{i}))
such that
x^{\prime}-\bigoplus_{i}\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(b)\in\ker\big(\oplus_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}A}\big)
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{A}(x-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(b))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{A}(x-s(x^{\prime})+s(x^{\prime}-\oplus_{i}\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(b)))

\displaystyle=0+s^{\dagger}(\oplus_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}(A)}(x^{\prime}-\oplus_{i}\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(b)))

\displaystyle=0.

So
x-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(b)
lies in the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{A}
as needed.
∎

Lemma 8.20
.

For
i=1,\dots,n
, let
B_{i}
be a connected,
3
-coconnected CW complex. Let
B:=P_{2}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i})
. Let
f^{i}\colon B_{i}\to B
be the composition
B_{i}\hookrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B_{i}\to B
.
Let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(B)
and
G_{i}:=\pi_{1}(B_{i})
for each
i
.
Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}\Gamma(H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H_{2}(B_{i},\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(f^{i}_{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}}

commutes, where
\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is as in
Definition

8.15
.

Proof.

By naturality of Whitehead’s exact sequence
[
73
]
, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(H_{2}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(H_{2}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(\widetilde{f}^{i}_{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}^{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

commutes.
We use the isomorphisms
H_{*}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z})\cong H_{*}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})
and
H_{*}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})\cong H_{*}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Under these,
\widetilde{f}^{i}_{*}\colon H_{2}(\widetilde{B}_{i};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{2}(\widetilde{B};\mathbb{Z})
agrees with the composition

H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})\xrightarrow{u}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})\cong H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\xrightarrow{f^{i}_{*}}H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi),

where we omit
\Res_{G_{i}}^{\pi}
from the notation for readability.
Also inverting the vertical isomorphisms, we obtain the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H_{2}(B_{i},\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(f^{i}_{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}\circ u}

By naturality, the diagram is
G_{i}
-equivariant, and thus induces the diagram from the statement by applying
\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}
, and also noticing that by doing so
\Gamma(u)
agrees with
\psi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}
.
∎

Lemma 8.21
.

For
i=1,\dots,n
, let
B_{i}
be a connected,
3
-coconnected CW complex. Let
B:=P_{2}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i})
. Let
f^{i}\colon B_{i}\to B
be the composition
B_{i}\hookrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B_{i}\to B
.
Let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(B)
and
G_{i}:=\pi_{1}(B_{i})
for each
i
. For each
i
, let
\overline{w}_{i}
denote the composition
G_{i}\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
. Then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{\overline{w}_{i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}}

commutes, where
\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is as in
Definition

8.15
.

Proof.

We can extend the diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{\overline{w}_{i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\mapsto\langle-,-\cap x\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{i}_{*}}

The right hand square then commutes by naturality of the Kronecker product since
\Theta_{B}(f^{i}_{*}(x))=\langle-,-\cap f^{i}_{*}(x)\rangle
.
To see that the left hand triangle commutes, note that for all
x\in H_{4}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{\overline{w}_{i}})
,
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})
and
g,g^{\prime}\in\pi
we have

\langle g\otimes\alpha,(g^{\prime}\otimes\beta)\cap x\rangle=g\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle\overline{g^{\prime}}=\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}}(\Theta_{B_{i}}(x))(g\otimes\alpha,g^{\prime}\otimes\beta).\qed

Proposition 8.22
.

Let
\pi
be a
3
-manifold group and let
w\colon\pi\to C_{2}
be a homomorphism such that
(\pi,w)
is admissible.
Then
(\pi,w)
has Property 4HL.

Proof.

Let
M
be a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
. We have to show that
(P_{2}(M),w)
has Property 4HL. By
Lemma

8.3
, it suffices to show this for some stabilisation of
M
. By
Lemma

3.13
, we can thus assume that
M=\#_{i}M_{i}
, where each
M_{i}
is a closed
4
-manifold with fundamental group
\pi_{1}(M_{i})=:G_{i}
either cyclic, isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2
, or a
PD_{3}
-group. Let
B_{i}:=P_{2}(M_{i})
and let
B:=P_{2}(M)\simeq P_{2}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i})
.
For each
i
, denote by
\overline{w}_{i}
the composition
G_{i}\hookrightarrow\pi\xrightarrow{w}C_{2}
.

Consider the following diagram with exact rows. The top left and the second-from-bottom left square are given in
Lemmas

8.20
and
8.21
, respectively. The maps
\varphi_{B_{i}}
,
\varphi_{B}
,
\Theta_{B_{i}}
, and
\Theta_{B}
are as in (
2.7
).
The map
\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is the map induced by
(f^{i}_{*}\circ\xi_{i}^{\Sigma_{2}})_{i}
, where the terms come from
Lemma

8.21
.
The second and third rows are part of the long exact sequences for the pair
(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i})
. The diagram commutes by naturality of
\varphi
,
\xi^{\Sigma_{2}}
and
\Theta
, together with
Lemmas

8.20
and
8.21
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}\Gamma(H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker(\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(H^{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker(\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{i}\Her^{\overline{w}_{i}}(H_{2}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})^{\star})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Her^{w}(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker(\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i}\Upsilon_{i}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vee_{i}\varphi_{B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vee_{i}\Theta_{B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus\ev^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

The vertical map second from the top on the right is an isomorphism by the relative Leray–Serre spectral sequence
[
66
]
*Theorem 15.27, Remark 2, p. 351 since
H_{j}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)=0
for
j<4
. The maps denoted by
\ev^{*}
are isomorphisms by
Proposition

5.9
since
\pi
is a
3
-manifold group. Note that the composition of the five maps in the middle column is the map
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
.

Note that

H_{3}\big(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi\big)\cong\bigoplus_{i}H_{3}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\cong\bigoplus_{i}\Ind_{G_{i}}^{\pi}H_{3}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i}),

and
0=\pi_{3}(B_{i})\to H_{3}(B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})
is surjective for each
i
by the Hurewicz theorem. Therefore
H_{3}\big(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi\big)=0
and the map
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)\to\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}G_{i})
on the second row is surjective.

Let
x\in\ker(H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))
be such that
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0
for all
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. We will show that
x
admits a lift to
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. This will complete the proof that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL.

Let
\overline{x}
be the image of
x
in
H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})

and let
\overline{z}
be the preimage of
\overline{x}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
under the isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Let
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
be a preimage of
\overline{z}
and let
y\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
be a preimage under the top middle isomorphism. Let
u
denote the image of
y
in
\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
. By the definition of the map
\Theta_{B}
, the element
x
maps trivially to
\Her^{w}(H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
. Therefore,
z
maps to zero in
\coker(\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}})
, which implies that
u
lies in the image of the map
\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}}
. Consider the image
v
of
y
in
\Her^{w}(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})
under the map
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
. Using the vertical isomorphisms between the two bottom rows of the diagram, we see that
v
lies in the image of
\Xi^{\Sigma_{2}}
, since
u
lies in the image of
\widehat{\Xi}^{\Sigma_{2}}
.

By
Lemma

8.19
, there exists
\widetilde{b}\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\bigoplus_{i}\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
such that
y-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(\widetilde{b})\in\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}\Gamma(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi))
maps trivially to
\Her^{w}(H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)^{\dagger})
.
In other words
y-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(\widetilde{b})
is in the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
. We know from
Corollary

7.10
that in our setting the kernel of
\mathcal{B}_{H_{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)}
equals the kernel of
\varphi_{B}
. Therefore, the element
y-\Psi^{\Sigma_{2}}(\widetilde{b})
also maps trivially to
H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Mapping further to
H_{4}(B,\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
, it follows that
\overline{z}=0
. This implies that
\overline{x}
is zero, so
x
lies in the image of
H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Let
\widetilde{x}\in H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}^{w})
be a preimage of
x
.

We know that each individual
(B_{i},w|_{\pi_{1}(B_{i}})
has Property 4HL by
Lemmas

8.8
,
8.9
,
8.10
, and
8.13
. Therefore by
Lemma

8.5
,
(\bigvee_{i}B_{i},w)
has Property 4HL as well. We will apply this momentarily. First we check that
\widetilde{x}
satisfies the desired conditions. We note that
\widetilde{x}
lies in
\ker(H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})\to H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w}))
, since the map
\bigvee_{i}B\to B\pi
factors through
B
and since
x
maps to
0
in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
. Moreover, for any
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
, we have

\langle\alpha,\beta\cap\widetilde{x}\rangle=\langle\alpha,\beta\cap\iota_{*}(x)\rangle=\langle\alpha,\iota_{*}(\iota^{*}(\beta)\cap x)\rangle=\langle\iota^{*}(\alpha),\iota^{*}(\beta)\cap x\rangle=0,

where the final equality follows by our assumption on
x
. Now we can apply the fact that
(\bigvee_{i}B,w)
has Property 4HL to conclude that
\widetilde{s}
admits a lift
\widetilde{t}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(\bigvee_{i}B_{i};\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. The image of
\widetilde{s}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
is the required lift of
x
. It follows that
(B,w)
has Property 4HL as needed.
∎

9.
Proof of
Theorem

1.4

With all our preliminary results in hand, we are finally able to prove the main theorem.
We recall the statement for the convenience of the reader.

Theorem
1.4
.

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be closed
4
-manifolds with fundamental group
\pi
and orientation character
w
, such that
\pi
is a
3
-manifold group and
(\pi,w)
is admissible.

Then every isomorphism
\smash{Q(M)\xrightarrow{\cong}Q(M^{\prime})}
between the quadratic
2
-types of
M
and
M^{\prime}
is realised by a homotopy equivalence.
In particular,
M
and
M^{\prime}
are homotopy equivalent if and only if they have isomorphic quadratic
2
-types.
Here, homotopy equivalences are assume to be basepoint and local orientation preserving.

Proof.

We will apply
Corollary

2.8
.
Assume that
M
and
M^{\prime}
have isomorphic quadratic
2
-types. By definition this means that for
B
the Postnikov
2
-type of
M
(and equivalently of
M^{\prime}
), we have
3
-connected maps
f\colon M\to B
and
f^{\prime}\colon M^{\prime}\to B
inducing a given identification of the quadratic
2
-type. Let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(B)
.
By
Proposition

4.10
,
\pi_{2}(M)
and
\pi_{2}(M^{\prime})
are free as abelian groups, so
Theorem

2.4
(
1
) holds.
Theorem

2.4
(
3
) holds by
Corollary

7.10
.
Also by
Proposition

5.9
,
\ev^{*}
is injective (and in fact an isomorphism), so
Theorem

2.4
(
4
) holds.

Next we show that
M
and
M^{\prime}
satisfy
Theorem

2.4
(
2
), for
k=1
. Consider the element

x:=f_{*}[M]-(f^{\prime})_{*}[M^{\prime}]\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w}).

By
Corollary

3.23
, which applies since
M
and
M^{\prime}
have isomorphic quadratic
2
-types,
x
maps to the trivial element in
H_{4}(\pi;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
.
Moreover, since
M
and
M^{\prime}
have isomorphic equivariant intersection forms, we also know that
\langle\alpha,\beta\cap x\rangle=0
for every
\alpha,\beta\in H^{2}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
.
By
Proposition

8.22
,
\pi
has Property 4HL.
Then by Property 4HL, we conclude that
x\in H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}^{w})
is contained in the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{w}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\pi}H_{4}(B;\mathbb{Z}\pi)
. Therefore the given
M
and
M^{\prime}
satisfy
Theorem

2.4
(
2
), for
k=1
.

Since all the requirements of
Theorem

2.4
are satisfied, we see by
Corollary

2.8
that
M
and
M^{\prime}
are homotopy equivalent, via a homotopy equivalence inducing the desired maps
(f^{\prime})_{*}^{-1}\circ f_{*}\colon\pi_{1}(M)\to\pi_{1}(M^{\prime})
and
(f^{\prime})_{*}^{-1}\circ f_{*}\colon\pi_{2}(M)\to\pi_{2}(M^{\prime})
.
∎

10.
Applications to geometrically 2-dimensional and finite groups

In this section we reprove a couple of results of Hambleton, Kreck, and Teichner, in order to demonstrate the wide applicability of our methods. That is, as well as dealing with new fundamental groups, our methods recover previously known results.

10.1.
Geometrically 2-dimensional groups

In
[
25
]
*Theorem C, Hambleton, Kreck, and Teichner showed that closed, oriented
4
-manifolds with geometrically 2-dimensional fundamental group
\pi
that satisfies the Farrell-Jones conjecture, are classified up to
s
-cobordism by their quadratic
2
-type together with their Kirby-Siebenmann invariant and their
w_{2}
-type. It follows that such
4
-manifolds are classified up to homotopy equivalence by their quadratic
2
-type together with the
w_{2}
-type.

The
w_{2}
-type is determined by the homotopy type but, as we show in the next example, the
w_{2}
-type is not determined by the quadratic
2
-type. Thus the
w_{2}
-type has to be included in the data for a complete homotopy classification, and the analogue of the statement of
Theorem

1.4
does not hold for geometrically 2-dimensional groups.

Example 10.1
.

Let
S^{2}\to S^{2}\widetilde{\times}T^{2}\to T^{2}
be the unique
S^{2}
-bundle over
T^{2}
that is orientable but not spin. Then both
S^{2}\times T^{2}
and
S^{2}\widetilde{\times}T^{2}
